❯ So in this breakdown, which one can be done in advance with less panelty if   
seculation is used? If we have MCP, or some document loading, it belonng to     
which phase? Don't change anything                                              

● Which phases can speculation overlap?

  ┌─────────────┬───────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │    Phase    │ Speculatable? │                    Why                     │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │             │               │ SDK startup, model loading — predictable,  │
  │ init_s      │      Yes      │ doesn't depend on the plan content. Can    │
  │             │               │ start the moment you guess which agent is  │
  │             │               │ needed.                                    │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │             │               │ If you speculatively prefill the prompt    │
  │ thinking_s  │   Partially   │ (warm the KV cache), the model starts      │
  │             │               │ reasoning faster. But the reasoning itself │
  │             │               │  depends on the actual task.               │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │             │               │ This is the real work — reading files,     │
  │ tool_exec_s │      No       │ writing code, running commands. Can't do   │
  │             │               │ this until the agent decides what to do.   │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ post_s      │      No       │ Response assembly after tool use. Depends  │
  │             │               │ on tool results.                           │
  └─────────────┴───────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  So init_s is free to speculate — near zero penalty if the speculation is wrong
   (you just discard the initialized agent). thinking_s is partially 
  speculatable via prompt prefill — you warm the cache with the system prompt +
  likely context, saving the prefill portion of thinking time.

  Where do MCP and document loading fall?

  MCP server connections, document loading, dataset fetching — these happen in
  tool_exec_s. They're triggered by the agent's tool calls (Read, Bash, MCP
  tools). But here's the key insight:

  You can speculate the MCP connection and document loading during init_s / 
  thinking_s. For example:

  Norm:   [init] → [thinking] → [MCP connect + load doc] → [process doc]
                                  ↑ tool_exec_s starts here

  Specul: [init] → [thinking] → [process doc]  ← MCP already connected, doc
  already loaded
             ↑ start MCP connect here (overlapped)

  If the planner's output hints "step 2 needs hpc_coder with file X", you can:
  1. Start the MCP server connection during the planner's generation
  2. Pre-load the document/dataset into memory
  3. When the agent actually calls Read/MCP, it hits a warm cache

  This is resource pre-staging — the speculation isn't about the agent's
  decision, it's about the infrastructure the agent will need. The penalty for
  wrong speculation is just wasted I/O (loaded a document nobody needed), which
  is cheap compared to the latency saved.

  For your paper, the breakdown maps to:

  Speculation Window = init_s + thinking_s (prefill portion)
  Resource Pre-staging Window = init_s + thinking_s (full, for MCP/doc loading)
  Actual Execution = tool_exec_s + post_s (cannot speculate)

  This is your §3 cost model: speculation profit = overlapped time, penalty =
  wasted init + wasted I/O.

❯ For resource pre-staging, is it doable in practice? how? Don't change         
existing stuff, just answer question                                            

● Yes, it's doable. Here's how:

  The key insight: you don't need to know exactly what the agent will do — you
  just need to know what resources it will likely need. That's predictable from
  the agent type.

  Practical mechanisms:

  1. MCP server pre-connection — When the planner starts generating and you see
  "hpc_coder" in the partial output, immediately open the MCP connection. MCP
  servers are long-lived processes — connecting takes 1-3s (subprocess spawn,
  handshake), but once connected, tool calls are fast. You can keep a pool of
  pre-warmed MCP connections.
  2. Document/context pre-loading — If you know hpc_coder will need the output
  from hpc_planner (from depends_on), you can pre-serialize that context into
  the prompt template while the planner is still finishing other steps. This is
  just string formatting — cheap but still blocking in the norm path.
  3. Model pre-loading (Ollama) — Ollama keeps models in GPU memory with a TTL.
  You can send a dummy request (num_predict: 0) to ensure the model is loaded
  before the agent needs it. This saves 2-10s of cold-start time.
  4. KV cache warming — Send the system prompt + likely context as a prefill
  request. The KV cache stays warm for subsequent requests with the same prefix.
   This works on both Ollama (via /api/generate with num_predict: 0) and Claude
  (prompt caching).

  What's already done in industry:

  - Claude prompt caching — Anthropic caches prompt prefixes server-side. If you
   send the same system prompt repeatedly, subsequent requests skip prefill.
  This is automatic but you can optimize for it by keeping system prompts
  stable.
  - Ollama keep-alive — keep_alive parameter controls how long models stay
  loaded. Set it high for benchmark runs.
  - MCP connection pooling — Not built into the SDK yet, but trivial to
  implement: maintain a dict of {server_name: connection} and reuse across agent
   calls.

  What's NOT doable:

  - Pre-executing tool calls (you don't know which file to read)
  - Pre-generating agent output (that's the actual work)
  - Speculating on tool results (depends on runtime state)

  For your paper: resource pre-staging is the low-hanging fruit of speculative
  dispatch. It's low-risk (wasted connection/load is cheap), high-reward (saves
  1-5s per agent), and implementable today without any model changes. The harder
   speculation — predicting which agents to launch — is where your cost model
  and learner come in.

❯ Based on this, is your speculative overlap table still correct if you can     
pre-staging reesource?                                                          
● No, the table needs updating. With resource pre-staging, tool_exec_s becomes
  partially speculatable:

  ┌─────────────┬──────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┐
  │    Phase    │   Without pre-staging    │     With pre-staging     │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
  │ init_s      │    Fully speculatable    │    Fully speculatable    │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
  │ thinking_s  │ Partially (prefill only) │ Partially (prefill only) │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
  │ tool_exec_s │     Not speculatable     │  Partially speculatable  │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
  │ post_s      │     Not speculatable     │     Not speculatable     │
  └─────────────┴──────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────┘

  The tool_exec_s breakdown is actually two sub-phases:

  tool_exec_s = resource_setup + actual_operation

  ┌───────────────┬─────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┐
  │   Sub-phase   │       Example       │          Speculatable?           │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ MCP connect   │ Spawn server,       │        Yes — pre-connect         │
  │               │ handshake           │                                  │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Model load    │ Load weights to GPU │          Yes — pre-load          │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ File          │ Glob/Grep for files │   Yes — if workspace is known    │
  │ discovery     │                     │                                  │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ File read     │ Read source file    │   Partially — if file path is    │
  │               │                     │           predictable            │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Code          │ LLM writes code     │                No                │
  │ generation    │                     │                                  │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ File write    │ Write output to     │                No                │
  │               │ disk                │                                  │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Bash          │ Compile/run code    │                No                │
  │ execution     │                     │                                  │
  └───────────────┴─────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┘

  So the revised speculation window is:

  Fully speculatable:    init_s + resource_setup (subset of tool_exec_s)
  Partially speculatable: thinking_s (prefill portion)
  Not speculatable:      actual_operation (subset of tool_exec_s) + post_s

  This is actually a stronger result for your paper — the speculation window is
  wider than just init + thinking. You can argue three tiers of speculation:

  1. Tier 1 — Zero penalty: agent init, MCP pre-connect, model pre-load
  2. Tier 2 — Low penalty: KV cache prefill, workspace pre-creation, context
  pre-serialization
  3. Tier 3 — Not speculatable: LLM generation, tool execution, result assembly

  Your hook instrumentation measures the total envelope. The cost model in §3
  would decompose tool_exec_s further into setup vs operation to quantify the
  tier 1+2 overlap window.
